# Native parity gap analysis: `expo/` against the web app

**Baseline:** re-audited **2026-10-14** against commit `226c0df9` — the tip of `beta` in the worktree this re-audit ran in (`beta` promotes to `main`; see AGENTS.md "Channels"). The audit container held a depth-1 clone of `beta` alone, so `main` was not fetched and nothing below was taken from this document's own earlier text: every claim in sections 2 and 3 was re-read in that tree. **That pass re-audited sections 2 and 3 only** (board card `6acf4dbdeabd988f96d40dca`). **A second pass on the same day re-audited section 5's 180 rows, re-ran section 4 and regenerated section 13's ticket index, against `b405bc1e`** — the tip of `beta` after that first pass merged (board card `6acf6999eabd988f96d40e77`); its evidence is cited against `b405bc1e` and was read in its own container, again from a depth-1 clone of `beta` alone. The first audit was commit `82f8b4bc`, audited 2026-09-28 and written 2026-09-29, and sections 1 and 6-12 still describe it.

**What moved between `82f8b4bc` and `226c0df9` (2026-09-28 → 2026-10-14).** Eleven of the twelve headline findings and nineteen of the twenty foundation defects are fixed; the twentieth (row 12) is fixed in part and retired in part by the 2026-09-30 decision to use no Expo-hosted service. Two things in section 2 are NOT fixed and stay on the page: `verifyAuth` still never reads the User row (finding 9), and five of section 2's own "not a gap" claims have become live work (finding 11). The route tree moved under the whole document: `expo/app` now holds `(app)/`, `(auth)/`, `_layout.tsx`, `index.tsx`, `onboarding.tsx`, `+native-intent.tsx` and `_stories.tsx`, and the tabs live one group deeper, under `(app)`. Every tab path in sections 2, 3 and 5 was corrected on 2026-10-14; the only cited route with no successor is the nutrition day view, which folded into the nutrition tab root (see section 5). **Section 5 was the stale part when this paragraph was written** — its paths were current but its Status cells were still the 2026-09-28 verdicts — and the second pass of 2026-10-14 re-checked every one of them against `b405bc1e`: 139 Parity, 24 Web-only, 12 Partial, 5 Missing, zero Broken, six rows flagged as device or human facts, with the tally recomputed from the cells at the end of that section.

**Scope:** every surface of the Next.js web app (`webapp/`, the source of truth) compared with the React Native app (`expo/`, Expo SDK 56 at the first audit and **SDK 57 since** — `expo/package.json:21`, re-checked 2026-10-14; expo-router, NativeWind 4) and the zod client only the native app consumes (`shared/api-client`). The result is 206 tickets (122 for the first store release) under EPIC Native App Parity (board card 6ab027beee5866d95d14dea5), keyed NP-001 to NP-206. This document supersedes the early gap analysis catalogue (retired by NP-116).

**Decisions it honours:** redbtn publishes the apps (9/13). No In-App Purchase: Plus is sold only by external link to the web checkout on the US storefront, and prices always come from the API (9/20, Apple 3.1.1(a)). Jon archived the iOS wrapper feature on 9/27, and on 9/28 George decided the native app ships on both stores, replacing the planned iOS wrapper and the Android Trusted Web Activity alike, so it is the first binary on both listings. (The slice analysis ran before that decision and treated Android v1 as the TWA; the plan, the waves and this document were corrected for it on 9/29.) Chat and community are on hold for the first store release (9/28), so the native v1 hides them.

**On the board:** every NP key is a ticket under [EPIC: Native App Parity](https://board.redbtn.io/b/6a70c4ea2fff468f8e253a89?card=6ab027beee5866d95d14dea5) on the Become redBoard (written 2026-09-29); the ticket index at the end links each one. Keys NP-205 and NP-206 and the Android release in NP-201 were added after the slice analysis, for the 9/28 decision to ship the native app on both stores.

## 1. How this was produced

- **Three slices, one owner each.** Slice A covered the platform, account, push, native-only capabilities and release; slice B training, the dashboard, streaks, the calendar and progress; slice C nutrition, Mind, community and chat, admin and AI. Each analyst read the current web app at `82f8b4bc` (not the May plan), the matching `expo/` and `shared/api-client` code, the board's Native App Parity and App & Play Store Readiness trees, and the 2026-07-14 state-of-play report, and cited `path:line` for every claim.
- **Every "native lacks X" claim is backed by a grep of `expo/` and `shared/` that finds nothing.** The greps that carry the headline gaps, all over `expo/app`, `expo/components`, `expo/lib` and `shared/api-client/src` unless noted, and all with zero hits: `expo-camera|CameraView|BarCodeScanner|barcode`; `expo-image-picker|ImagePicker|launchCamera`; `speech|Speech|MirrorScene`; `Haptics|expo-haptics|Vibration|Share.share`; `/api/ai/|runAiTask|allowanceTicket|EntitlementRequired`; `ai-consent|aiConsent|AI_CONSENT`; `entitlement|Entitlement|gateFrom|UpgradeSheet|TierGate|requiresTier`; `/api/mind/|SessionPlayer|mindXP|composeSession` (only the tz prefix list matches); `/api/report|/api/block|blockedUsers`; `<Link` (no link component anywhere); `AppState`; `expo-font|useFonts|fontFamily`; `sentry|bugsnag` (also over `webapp/`); and `expo-notifications`, `expo-dev-client` and `expo-updates` in `expo/package.json`.
- **A verifier re-checked each slice.** It re-ran the commands, checked that every cited path exists and every cited line range is inside its file (216 paths in slice A alone), read the cited behaviour, corrected wrong premises (for example: plans do auto-promote on the web; windowed allowances key on the stored timezone, not the request's; the onboarding finish writes `PUT /api/goals`; beta shares production's database), split tickets bigger than about three days, and added gaps the first pass missed, including the Metro bundling failure.
- **This synthesis merged the three verified slices.** Overlaps got one owner (the timezone fix, the web-path resolver, the signed-in web hand-off, the logic-sharing decision, the chat flag, the refusal classifier); every dependency points at a ticket key or a board card; the graph is acyclic, no ticket depends on a later wave, and no v1 ticket depends on a non-v1 ticket. A completeness pass added eight tickets no slice owned: the day rollover on return to the foreground (NP-035), recording the member's timezone when an app opens (NP-070), four training product decisions (NP-028), a web sharing hole (NP-029), App Store screenshots from the native build (NP-119), the offline live workout (NP-191), the web's typeface (NP-160), and last-known data with offline and error states (NP-036). Six new stories hold work no existing story fitted: crash reporting, OTA updates and the version gate (NS-01); theme, typeface and accessibility (NS-02); onboarding (NS-03); the food log on MealLog (NS-04); the calendar (NS-05); and the live workout (NS-06), split out of the training audit story, which had about 25 tickets. Keys run in order of wave, then priority, then the story order of the board tree, with new stories after their feature's existing ones.
- **Read-only.** No commits, pushes, board writes, messages or deploys, and no production data. The only commands run were `npm ci`, `tsc`, ESLint, Jest, `expo install --check`, expo-doctor and a Metro export, inside the worktree's `expo/` and `shared/api-client` or an isolated copy of them.

## 2. Headline findings

**Re-read line by line against `226c0df9` on 2026-10-14.** Each entry keeps its 2026-09-28 subject and says what the tree holds today, with a `path:line` that exists on that commit. The audit container held a depth-1 clone, so `git log` could name no merge commit or PR number: a fix is evidenced by the code, the NP key stamped in it and the acceptance test that landed beside it. Eleven of the twelve findings have changed; nothing below is carried over on trust.

1. **Fixed — the app builds, bundles and launches.** "The native app cannot be built or launched as it stands" is no longer true. React matches the renderer (`expo/package.json:54,56` — react 19.2.3, react-native 0.86.3) on SDK 57 (`:21`, expo `~57.0.26`), which also retires the Hermes regression that was "fixed only on SDK 57"; Metro reaches the shared client and ships exactly one copy of `zod` (`expo/metro.config.js:18,24,37-48`); the assets exist (`expo/assets/`: `icon.png`, `adaptive-icon.png`, `splash-icon.png`, `splash-icon-light.png`, `fonts/`, `sounds/`, wired at `expo/app.json:9,91,151-161`); and CI bundles the app on every native PR — `npx expo export --platform ios` walks the whole module graph (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:304-305`) after typecheck, lint, Jest and `npx expo install --check` (`:234-296`). The EAS project the finding wanted is not missing but **banned**: no Expo-hosted service, no `eas.json`, no `expo-updates` (George, 2026-09-30; `expo/RELEASE.md:5-13`), enforced as CI's first native step by `expo/scripts/check-no-ota.mjs` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:213-214`) and by `expo/__tests__/storeDistribution.test.ts`. The Builder & QA lane reports a 2026-10-13 build of `8f65c1ba` installed on a Galaxy S23 (APK hash match, 14 screens captured, iOS ok); that device run lives outside this repository and was **not** verifiable from the audit container — what is verifiable here is that every repo-side blocker the finding listed is gone. (NP-004, NP-006, NP-005, NP-014, NP-040)
2. **Fixed — a native date is the member's local day.** `tz` on the wire is minutes west of UTC, the number the server actually parses, and is never faked as 0 (`shared/api-client/src/tz.ts:1-33`); it rides the query string on reads and the JSON body on writes, with the IANA zone travelling separately as `tzZone` (`shared/api-client/src/apiFetch.ts:58-60,82-87`; server side `webapp/lib/dayWindow.ts:60-70`). The zone is also recorded on launch and on the first foreground of a new local day, not only by a workout save (`expo/components/TimezoneReporter.tsx`, mounted at `expo/app/_layout.tsx:197`). (NP-009, NP-070)
3. **Fixed — the launch path keeps the session and the tab bar is the web's.** One session above every route (`expo/app/_layout.tsx:187`), held in SecureStore under `become.session` rather than a memory store (`:118,131`; `expo/lib/auth/secureStoreToken.ts:21-54`, one key per purpose, so the Health toggle can no longer land on the JWT); the launch decision is a route the router mounts only on `/`, so a cold start from `/verify?token=…` is never overruled by sign-in (`expo/app/index.tsx:38-100`); and the bar is five buttons with Home in the middle, chat behind a flag and calendar without a button (`expo/app/(app)/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:58-64,179-182`). Rendered rather than asserted: `expo/__tests__/navigation-shell.test.tsx:128-132` reads the bar out of the real route tree. (NP-002, NP-003, NP-001, NP-351, NP-315)
4. **Fixed — all five wrong-data paths.** Food logging sends an external hit through the ungated import and then `POST /api/meal-logs`, scaled per serving (`expo/lib/nutrition/foodImport.ts:11`, `expo/lib/nutrition/mealLogActions.ts:144,188,209`, `expo/lib/nutrition/foodMath.ts:189-224`) — NP-011; a swap saves the replacement's slug and keeps the original only as provenance (`expo/lib/live/workoutSave.ts:50-52,128-156`) — NP-083; schedule settings submit `{ programId, trainingDays }` and no start date (`expo/app/(app)/(tabs)/calendar/settings.tsx:158-171`) — NP-069; enrolment sends a device-local `startDate` and lands on schedule setup (`expo/app/(app)/(tabs)/programming/[id]/index.tsx:200-207`, `expo/lib/programs/enrollment.ts:18-54`) — NP-074; and the weight box is unit-aware on both sides (`expo/components/profile/ProfileSettingsScreen.tsx:137-150,221-243,514-580`; `webapp/app/api/weight/route.ts:196-200,314,363`) — NP-048/NP-017. Beta still shares production's database, so the warning that travelled with this finding still holds.
5. **Fixed — the account and money layer is in the app.** Terms are agreed at sign-up and sent with the registration (`expo/app/(auth)/login.tsx:1082-1149`, posted at `:251`); a blocking consent gate sits between the session and onboarding and asks for Terms and for AI consent (`expo/components/auth/ConsentGate.tsx:57-70`, mounted at `expo/app/(app)/_layout.tsx:64`); there is an entitlements store (`expo/lib/entitlements/store.ts`), an upgrade sheet mounted once above every route and fed by the plan-gate classifier (`expo/app/_layout.tsx:226-229,248`, `expo/lib/entitlements/upgradeSheet.ts`), a plan page (`expo/app/(app)/plan.tsx`), prices from the API (`webapp/app/api/billing/plans/route.ts`) and a checkout that starts in-app with the member's token and returns to `/plan` rather than a signed-out browser page (`expo/lib/entitlements/billing.ts:97`, `expo/app/index.tsx:84-98`). (NP-045, NP-046, NP-049, NP-050, NP-052, NP-053, NP-054)
6. **Fixed — push registers and sends on both ends.** `expo-notifications` is installed (`expo/package.json:40`) and configured (`expo/app.json:260`); registration posts the RAW APNs/FCM device token to a route that exists (`expo/lib/push/nativePush.ts:95-107,147`; `webapp/app/api/notifications/subscribe/route.ts:16-22`); the tap router switches on `data.url`, the same input the web's service worker reads, not on categories (`expo/lib/push/tapRouter.ts:12,88`); and the server sends the native rows instead of skipping them (`webapp/lib/pushNotification.ts:124-157`, through `webapp/lib/push/nativeSend.ts`). Members with no stored zone are no longer the normal case (finding 2). **New gap noticed, not carded:** `expo/lib/push/registerPushToken.ts:30` still posts an EXPO push token to `/api/push/subscribe` and neither end exists any more — `webapp/app/api/push/` is absent and nothing but `expo/__tests__/registerPushToken.test.ts` imports the helper. (NP-030, NP-063, NP-064, NP-065, NP-066, NP-067, NP-068, NP-070)
7. **Fixed — the headline features are present natively.** Camera: `expo-camera` is installed (`expo/package.json:27`) with the purpose strings in place (`expo/app.json:20-23`) and is used by the barcode scanner (`expo/components/nutrition/BarcodeScanner.tsx`) and the Mind mirror (`expo/components/mind/session/scenes/MirrorScene.tsx`). AI: one run client that carries the signed follow-up ticket and polls without charging (`expo/lib/ai/runClient.ts:18-19,719`). Mind: a session player with eighteen scenes including speech and the mirror (`expo/components/mind/session/SessionPlayer.tsx`, `expo/components/mind/session/scenes/`), and a home with levels, chapters, XP and a resumable session (`expo/app/(app)/(tabs)/mind/index.tsx:83-95,131-135`) — it is no longer "a mood picker". Whether Mind now MATCHES the web was **not** re-audited in this pass; its section 5 rows still carry their 2026-09-28 statuses. (NP-059, NP-088, NP-089, NP-090, NP-038, NP-097, NP-098, NP-103, NP-099, NP-100, NP-101)
8. **Fixed — the shared client describes today's API and a test fails when the web moves.** `shared/api-client/src/schemas/` now holds 26 files, account, consent, entitlements, dashboard, mind, ai and notifications among them, and the nutrition schemas were rewritten against the real server — a recipe's per-serving macros are `totalsPerServing`, which is what `webapp/models/Recipe.ts` stores (`shared/api-client/src/schemas/nutrition.ts:6-32`). Twelve contract suites run in `verify` on every PR and fail when a response shape moves (`webapp/tests/unit/contract/`, e.g. `np015Routes.test.ts`, `np024Nutrition.test.ts`, `np037Mind.test.ts`). (NP-015, NP-016)
9. **Partly still true — the five web bugs are fixed; the deletion question is not.** Fixed: a save resolves the oldest outstanding slot and matches today's slot through `slotDateKey`, not by UTC proximity (`webapp/app/api/workouts/route.ts:680-725`) — NP-027; Begin sends `tz` and the route reads it, with a compat fallback for an old bundle (`webapp/components/mind/MindJourney.tsx:281-285`, `webapp/app/api/mind/session/route.ts:369-373`) — NP-031; a completing save replayed after midnight is matched by its own `attemptId` with no date bound, so it rewrites one log instead of inserting a second (`webapp/app/api/workouts/route.ts:500-520`) — NP-139; `POST /api/share` checks `canMemberOpenProgram` before publishing a program by id (`webapp/app/api/share/route.ts:47-53`) — NP-029; and the dashboard's Quick add link is read — `?quickAdd=true` opens the sheet (`webapp/components/nutrition/NutritionSummaryCard.tsx:198`, `webapp/app/dashboard/nutrition/page.tsx:169-171`) — NP-149. **Still true:** `verifyAuth` never reads the User row (`webapp/lib/auth.ts:156-189` — it verifies the JWT, checks the scope allow-list and returns the claims), so a token stays good until its own `exp` whatever the row says; `POST /api/auth/logout` bumps `widgetTokenVersion` and reaches read-only widgets tokens only (`expo/lib/auth/AuthProvider.tsx:21-26`). The open question stands.
10. **Fixed — chat is hidden on native behind one flag.** The chat screens are registered with the navigator only while `EXPO_PUBLIC_COMMUNITY_ENABLED` is on, so in a production build `/(tabs)/chat` resolves nowhere and lands on Home (`expo/app/(app)/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:179-181`, `expo/lib/flags/communityEnabled.ts`); the web still shows chat, community, groups and events as admin-only. Report and block still wait for the web. (NP-032)
11. **Partly false — three of its "not a gap" items are live work today, and its counts are stale.** Still true: `MindHub.tsx`, `VisionTab.tsx`, `JournalTab.tsx`, `MeditationTab.tsx` and `SleepTab.tsx` sit in `webapp/components/mind/` with no importer anywhere under `webapp/app` or `webapp/components` (grep, 2026-10-14), and `/dashboard/insights/[metricId]` is still a stub (`webapp/app/dashboard/insights/[metricId]/page.tsx:1-2`). No longer true: `/api/ai/mind/generate` has a live caller (`webapp/components/mind/VisionBuilder.tsx:140`), so has `/api/ai/nutrition/product` (`webapp/lib/nutrition/aiEngine.ts:122`), and `/dashboard/customize` is not legacy but the live tile editor persisting through `PATCH /api/dashboard/pinned-tiles` (`webapp/app/dashboard/customize/page.tsx:1-3`) — and it IS ported, from the dashboard's Customize tiles control (`expo/components/DashboardScreen.tsx:629`, `expo/__tests__/dashboardCustomizeNP157.test.tsx`, NP-157). The status tally ("30 Broken, 28 Partial, 98 Missing and 24 Web-only") no longer matches even this document: section 5's own cells read 94 Missing, 30 Partial, 28 Broken, 24 Web-only, 2 Parity, 1 Shipped and 1 Done across its 180 rows — and those cells were **not** re-audited here. **Superseded the same day:** the second pass (card `6acf6999eabd988f96d40e77`) re-audited all 180 cells against `b405bc1e` and the tally recomputed from them is **139 Parity, 24 Web-only, 12 Partial, 5 Missing and zero Broken** (end of section 5). Two of this finding's own claims moved under it within hours: the five legacy Mind components were DELETED from `webapp/components/mind/` on 2026-10-14 (PR #2007), so "they sit there with no importer" is no longer the right description, and `/api/ai/mind/generate` has no live caller again — `VisionBuilder.tsx` lost its last importer with `VisionTab.tsx` (see section 5's web-only rows).
12. **Out of date — the size of the job cannot be read from this document any more.** The ticket index stops at NP-210 (210 rows, 125 marked store-v1) while `main` carries work keyed far past it: NP-382 is the highest key in the tree and 372 distinct NP keys appear across `expo/` and `AGENTS.md` (grep, 2026-10-14; e.g. NP-368 at `expo/app/(app)/(tabs)/calendar/settings.tsx:58`, NP-351 at `expo/app/(app)/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:29`). Both prerequisites this finding named now have artefacts in the repository: the Apple Team ID is real in the AASA file (`webapp/public/.well-known/apple-app-site-association:5-7`) and `assetlinks.json` carries a SHA-256 fingerprint rather than a placeholder (`webapp/public/.well-known/assetlinks.json:9-11`). The 122-ticket / 192-engineer-day estimate and the 12-ticket critical path are therefore **not** restated: they need a fresh count against the board, which this pass did not do and will not guess. **Half-closed the same day:** section 13 now carries a second, generated table for every NP key above NP-210 that the tree actually contains — 166 of them, up to NP-382, each with where it lives and the line that names it (card `6acf6999eabd988f96d40e77`). The board columns (wave, store v1, size, priority, story) are still left to the board, for the same reason this finding gives.

## 3. Foundation defects

The twenty rows below were read at `82f8b4bc` on 2026-09-28 and **re-read one at a time against `226c0df9` on 2026-10-14**. The Evidence column is now the evidence on `226c0df9` — every path and line in it exists on that commit — and the State column says whether the defect survives. No row has been deleted: a fixed row keeps its number and its subject so the history stays readable. The audit container held a depth-1 clone, so no merge commit or PR number could be read from `git log`; a fix is named by its NP key and by the file (and, where one exists, the acceptance test) that carries it. Nineteen rows are fixed as described; one (row 12) is fixed in part and retired in part, by the decision that banned Expo-hosted services.

| # | Defect (2026-09-28) | Evidence on `226c0df9` (2026-10-14) | State on 2026-10-14 | Ticket |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | React does not match the renderer | `expo/package.json:54,56` (react 19.2.3, react-native 0.86.3), `:21` (expo `~57.0.26`) | **Fixed.** The two versions the renderer compares now agree, on SDK 57, so the "Incompatible React versions" throw on importing the real root layout is gone; CI bundles the app on every native PR (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:304-305`) | NP-004 |
| 2 | Metro cannot resolve the shared client | `expo/metro.config.js:18` (`watchFolders` includes `../shared`), `:24` (`resolver.nodeModulesPaths` names this app's `node_modules`), `:37-48` (shared `node_modules` blocked, so one copy of `zod` ships) | **Fixed.** `npx expo export --platform ios` is a required CI step and walks the whole graph, so an import Metro cannot resolve fails the PR (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:304-305`) | NP-006 |
| 3 | Dependencies out of line with the SDK | `.github/workflows/ci.yml:292-296` (`npx expo install --check` with `EXPO_OFFLINE=1`, a required step), `expo/package.json:21-81` (SDK 57 ranges throughout) | **Fixed.** Re-run in the audit container on `226c0df9` after `npm ci`: `EXPO_OFFLINE=1 npx expo install --check` → "Dependencies are up to date", exit 0 (the 9/28 run reported 13 mismatches). expo-doctor was not re-run | NP-004 |
| 4 | In-memory token stores at the root | `expo/app/_layout.tsx:118,131` (the JWT is `sessionStore`, SecureStore `become.session`), `:187` (one `AuthProvider` above every route), `expo/app/index.tsx:38-100` (the launch decision, mounted only on `/`) | **Fixed.** A launch restores the stored session, and a cold start on `/verify?token=…` or `/account/restore?…` is no longer replaced by sign-in | NP-002, NP-003 |
| 5 | Session handling per screen | `expo/lib/auth/useAuth.ts:22-24` (a read of one context), `expo/lib/auth/AuthProvider.tsx:219-222` (offline, DNS, a 500 — "says nothing about the session. Keep it"), `:462-465` (the refreshed token is stored) | **Fixed.** An offline launch stays signed in and the sliding session rolls, so a session no longer dies 30 days after sign-in | NP-002 |
| 6 | Health opt-in writes the session key | `expo/lib/auth/secureStoreToken.ts:21-54` (one named key per purpose, no default store), `expo/lib/health/opt-in.ts:17-26` (the store is a required argument), `expo/components/settings/HealthSyncSection.tsx:89` (`healthOptInSecureStore`, `become.optin.health`) | **Fixed.** Test: `expo/__tests__/optInKeepsSession.test.tsx` turns the toggle on and off and reads the JWT back; `expo/__tests__/secureStoreKeys.test.ts` pins the key map | NP-001 |
| 7 | Scaffold entry and a 20-slot tab bar | `expo/app/index.tsx:38-100` (the scaffold screen is gone; this is the launch redirect), `expo/app/(app)/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:58-64` (five tabs, Home third), `:179-182` (`chat` behind the community flag, `calendar` `href: null`), `expo/components/navigation/TabStack.tsx` (a Stack per tab, so a detail route is a push), `expo/app/(app)/(tabs)/profile/index.tsx` (Profile is a real route and a real button) | **Fixed.** `expo/__tests__/navigation-shell.test.tsx:128-132` renders the real route tree and reads five buttons; "No route named profile exists" cannot recur | NP-003, NP-013, NP-351 |
| 8 | Dead Start buttons | `expo/components/DashboardScreen.tsx:81-85` (`onStartWorkout` is a required prop), `expo/components/programs/WorkoutOverview.tsx:26-30` (`onStartLive` likewise) | **Fixed.** The `?? (() => {})` that swallowed the press is gone, and a route that forgets to pass one is a `tsc` failure (TS2741) | NP-026 |
| 9 | Wrong timezone contract | `shared/api-client/src/tz.ts:1-33` (minutes west of UTC, never an IANA name, never a fabricated 0), `shared/api-client/src/apiFetch.ts:58-60,82-87` (query string on reads, body on writes), `webapp/lib/dayWindow.ts:60-70` | **Fixed.** Same change as headline finding 2; `webapp/tests/unit/tzFamilyParity.test.ts` fails when a route family joins the server and not the client | NP-009 |

**What today's numbers change about the paragraph below.** Two of its three clauses are no longer true: a test does render
the real root and the real tab layout (`root-layout-smoke`, `navigation-shell`), and CI does bundle the app on every
native PR (`npx expo export --platform ios`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:304-305`). The third clause and the July warning
stand: a green suite is still not a working app, which is why every wave ends in a device demo — and why six rows in
section 5 are flagged as device facts rather than promoted.

The suite is green because no test renders the real root or tab layout or bundles the app, and many tests cover modules nothing mounts. The July report's warning still holds: a green suite is not a working app, so every wave below ends in a device demo, not a test run.

## 5. Side-by-side matrix

Status words: **Parity** (native matches the web), **Partial** (some of it exists natively), **Missing** (nothing native), **Broken** (native code exists but is wrong or unreachable), **Web-only** (stays on the web by decision). Ticket keys point to section 13. Paths are repository-relative.

> **Read this before you trust a row (2026-10-14, second pass).** **Every row below was re-audited against `b405bc1e`** — the tip of `beta` in the worktree this pass ran in — on 2026-10-14, row by row, in the order the tables appear. The first 2026-10-14 pass (sections 2 and 3, PR #2001) corrected **paths** here and nothing else: every `expo/app/(tabs)/…` citation became `expo/app/(app)/(tabs)/…`. This pass rewrote each row's **Status cell and its evidence** in the convention section 3 uses — the 2026-09-28 subject is kept word for word, and today's state and today's `path:line` are appended as **Re-audited 2026-10-14 (was X)**, so the old verdict is still readable beside the new one. Every `path:line` in this section was machine-checked to exist on that commit (1,439 citations; the only paths named that do not exist are the ones a cell explicitly says do not exist — a banned `eas.json`, a deleted helper, a route with no successor). **Six rows carry a flag instead of a promotion** where the answer is a device or human fact no container can settle; they are named under the tally at the end of this section. The tally itself is recomputed from these cells. Section 4 carries today's numbers beside the 2026-09-28 ones and section 13's index now runs to the highest key in the tree; sections 1 and 6 to 12 still describe the 2026-09-28 audit except where they say otherwise.

**Status tally, recomputed from the cells above on 2026-10-14.** Counted by splitting every row of the eighteen tables in this section on its Status cell — not by hand and not carried over: **139 Parity, 24 Web-only, 12 Partial, 5 Missing**, across 180 rows. **Zero Broken.** The 2026-09-28 verdicts these replace were 94 Missing, 30 Partial, 28 Broken, 24 Web-only, 2 Parity, 1 Shipped and 1 Done; the two words that were never in this section's own legend are gone — "Done" (shared pure logic) and "Shipped" (the meal schedule) are both Parity, counted and evidenced like everything else. No row moved backwards.

- **The 12 Partial rows, and what is missing in each:** assets and device builds (the API base URL is one hard-coded production constant, no per-profile config — NP-008); accessibility (no device pass with VoiceOver, TalkBack or the largest Dynamic Type); last-known data (the cache is opt-in per screen and defaults to off); program search (still query-only, no tag or level filter); the rest timer (question 25 unanswered and the lock-screen half is a device fact); the dumbbell labels (the one-or-two choice waits on the web, card 6aacc283); the PR line (NP-208's all-time rule is implemented on NEITHER client — the native port deliberately copied the web's last-session compare); Arsenal (Training Grounds and suggested actions are ported, the Arsenal page itself has no native route); 1:1 chat (correctly hidden in v1, so the two clients do not match by design); widgets (iOS surfaces now exist, no device evidence); Apple Health (iOS is still the adapter shape — the write half is NP-185/NP-186); store QA and submission (human and device work outside this repository).
- **The 5 Missing rows:** crash and error reporting (no provider, no root boundary — open question 4 is unanswered); community home, groups and events (deliberate, not v1); report and block (nothing on either client); Live Activity and the ongoing workout notification (nothing in the tree); the parity sign-off and migration plan (`expo/PARITY.md`, which NP-116 is meant to write out of this matrix, does not exist).
- **The 6 flagged rows — un-re-audited, with what was checked:** accessibility (the a11y modules and their tests were read; the VoiceOver / TalkBack / large-text run is not evidenced in this repository), account deletion and restore (the pending state and the AASA claim were read; "never run on a device" cannot be settled here), the rest timer (`restEndAlert`, `restSound`, `restDuration`, `useLiveBackGuard` and `expo-keep-awake` were read; whether the countdown survives a locked phone is a device fact), widgets (the Android and the new iOS widget code were read; no device or distribution exists here), biometric unlock (the real `expo-local-authentication` capability and the Settings section were read; whether Face ID unlocks on a device is a device fact), store QA and submission (the three documented defects were re-read and are fixed; the QA round and the submission are human facts). A flagged row is useful; a guessed one is the thing this re-audit exists to fix.

## 6. Schema drift

What `shared/api-client` says against what the live routes return at `82f8b4bc`. The web keeps its own hand copy in `webapp/lib/sharedApiTypes.ts`, used type-only by one route (`app/api/auth/me/route.ts:11`) and importing `zod` although `webapp/package.json` does not declare it; the contract test (NP-016) makes the two fail loudly when they disagree.

| Domain | Shared client today | Live contract | Consequence | Ticket |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Timezone | `src/tz.ts:6-43`: IANA name appended as `tz` on 8 path prefixes; nothing in bodies | `webapp/lib/dayWindow.ts:61-75`: minutes west of UTC, a non-number reads as 0; 19 route families read `tz`; bodies carry `tz`, workout saves also `tzZone` | Every native date is the UTC day; the member's zone is never stored from native | NP-009 |

## 7. Web-only (Tier 3) decisions

Tier 3 means the surface stays on the web and native shows at most a link. Admin and coach tooling defaults to Tier 3. Until NP-121 builds a signed-in hand-off, any web link from the app opens signed out, which is why the recommendation for v1 (NP-012) is to hide unported member surfaces rather than link to them.

## 9. Waves and the store-v1 critical path

Waves 0 to 3 are the first store release (store v1); waves 4 to 6 follow it. No ticket depends on a ticket in a later wave, and no v1 ticket depends on one outside v1. Wave 0 tickets start in To do; everything else starts in Backlog.

**Wave 0. Foundation: it builds, keeps its session and speaks the web's contract** (33 tickets, 32 for the store v1)

- Goal: The Expo app bundles and launches on real phones on SDK 57, keeps its session, shows only the real tabs, sends the timezone the server reads and understands its refusals; every v1 route has a schema under a webapp contract test; the native paths that write wrong data are fixed or fenced; the decisions that gate wave 1 are made; the three web bugs the audit found are fixed.
- Exit (device demo): On a real iPhone and a real Android phone, a development build installed from EAS opens on sign-in; a named test account signs in by email link, force-quits and relaunches straight into Home; the tab bar shows Workout, Mind, Home and Nutrition only; a mood logged at 9 pm Eastern shows on the web on the same day; logging a USDA food natively spends no custom-food slot.
- Tickets: NP-001, NP-002, NP-003, NP-004, NP-005, NP-006, NP-007, NP-008, NP-009, NP-010, NP-011, NP-012, NP-013, NP-014, NP-015, NP-016, NP-017, NP-018, NP-019, NP-020, NP-021, NP-022, NP-023, NP-024, NP-025, NP-026, NP-027, NP-028, NP-029, NP-030, NP-031, NP-208, NP-209, NP-210

**Wave 1. Account, money and plumbing** (39 tickets, 39 for the store v1)

- Goal: Everything around the pillars that a store build needs: sign-up and sign-in with the reviewer code, both consents, Settings with sign-out, legal links and units, onboarding with targets, plans with prices from the API and checkout in Safari with the way back, push end to end with the badge and taps, deep links and the real AASA, crash reporting, OTA updates and the version gate, last-known data and offline states, the day rollover, the shared-logic copies, the AI run client, the camera plumbing, chat hidden, and a production-signed build in TestFlight.
- Exit (device demo): On a TestFlight build on a real iPhone and an internal-testing build on a real Android phone, a new member signs up by email link, ticks the Terms and answers the AI question, finishes onboarding with calorie targets, allows notifications, receives the daily glance and taps it into the right screen, opens Plus checkout in Safari and returns to the app with the plan refreshed, relaunches in airplane mode to last-known data, then signs out and back in with the badge matching the web.
- Tickets: NP-032, NP-033, NP-034, NP-035, NP-036, NP-037, NP-038, NP-039, NP-040, NP-041, NP-042, NP-043, NP-044, NP-045, NP-046, NP-047, NP-048, NP-049, NP-050, NP-051, NP-052, NP-053, NP-054, NP-055, NP-056, NP-057, NP-058, NP-059, NP-060, NP-061, NP-062, NP-063, NP-064, NP-065, NP-066, NP-067, NP-068, NP-069, NP-070

**Critical path to submission.** The longest dependency chain among v1 tickets, weighting S, M and L as 1, 2 and 3 working days, the QA and member round as 5 and the submission as 2:

| Step | Key | Ticket | Size | Working days |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | NP-004 | Move expo/ to Expo SDK 57 and align every dependency so the app can launch | M | 2 |
| 2 | NP-002 | Make the session survive a restart: one auth provider on the secure store | M | 2 |
| 3 | NP-003 | Rebuild the navigation shell: guarded groups, a stack per tab, links kept on cold start | M | 2 |
| 4 | NP-008 | Per-profile API config and the first development build on a real iPhone and Android phone | M | 2 |
| 5 | NP-052 | Native upgrade sheet and tier gate; buying opens the web checkout in Safari | M | 2 |
| 6 | NP-097 | Replace the native mood-only Mind tab with the Mind home and its lock | L | 3 |
| 7 | NP-098 | Port the Mind session player with the state check and breath scenes | L | 3 |
| 8 | NP-099 | Add native speech recognition and word matching for the speak scene | M | 2 |
| 9 | NP-100 | Port the mirror scene with the front camera and live speech | M | 2 |
| 10 | NP-118 | Run device QA and the member round (TestFlight and Play internal testing), close blockers | M | 5 |
| 11 | NP-116 | Write the v1 parity checklist and the web-only list, and have Jon sign it off on a device | S | 1 |
| 12 | NP-120 | Submit the native build to the App Store, release in phases and keep a rollback path | S | 2 |
| | | Total before App Review | | 28 |

The chain runs through the foundation into the Mind session player, the hardest port in the app; the nutrition estimate chain (search, quantity picker, photo estimate, correction) is 27 working days and the training chain 27, so all three pillars sit on or next to the critical path. It assumes enough people to run the other chains beside it. With the work split across two or three engineers or agents, v1 takes longer than the path: about 192 engineer-days of v1 work (S=1, M=2, L=3 days; human and decision tickets excluded), about 38 engineer-weeks: 12 to 19 calendar weeks with two or three engineers or agents in parallel, never less than the 28-working-day critical path (about 5.6 weeks), plus one to two weeks of App Review.

**Prerequisites outside this epic, upstream of submission.** Store story 6ab0281d (App Store Connect record and Team ID) gates the EAS project and the first iPhone build; 6ab02822 (Play Console record and App Signing) gates App Links and the Play release; 6ab02884 (the reviewer demo login's server route) gates the native demo login; 6ab189bb (Manage billing on the web, in progress) gates the native plan page; 6ab028bd (processor list in the Privacy Policy) gates the App Privacy answers; 6ab02826 (store name), 6ab0282c (listing copy) and 6ab028a4 (the live purchase rehearsal on the web) gate submission.

**Web surfaces still moving, and where native waits.** The 10-day trial after onboarding (card 6ab1a322): Shipped in NP-129. Declining the tutorial and the per-page tutorial button (6ab6b821): NP-164 decided 10/8 (Jon chose (b) short native hub tour with decline, carded in NP-207 with own progress key; no native screen waits on tour state). Manage billing (6ab189bb): NP-053 depends on it. The cardio box layout in the live view (6abac4ab): NP-080 follows it when it lands. Other-metric exercises on the workout summary (6abac5bf): NP-086 builds to it. The pulsating "what to work on" tile (6ab6cb94): NP-192 depends on it. Also open: one or two dumbbells (6aacc283, NP-084 ports after it), the bodyweight default for custom exercises (6ab18beb, NP-169 after it), and the dark-mode chart ink bug (6a8d0c3c, NP-130 must not copy the fills). NP-028 decided 10/13 (Jon: PR meaning is all-time, kg members use kg throughout, Track and Live both ship): the summary's session-only "new personal record" at `WorkoutSummary.tsx:106-120` must become all-time first, carded as NP-208; the lb-only `dumbbellWeight.ts` must become unit-aware first, carded as NP-209 (both ahead of NP-080/NP-084/NP-086). NP-028 also decided 10/13 (Jon) that the locked-phone rest alert gives both sound and vibration when the timer ends, in Live and in Track alike: the web's vibrate-only Live alert and Track's missing rest timer must become sound-and-vibration-aware first, carded as NP-210 (ahead of NP-082). The permission-ask timing sub-question stayed open — NP-082 waits on it (question 25).

## 10. Risks

1. **The critical path starts with people, not code.** The Apple Team ID and App Store Connect record (6ab0281d), the Play Console record (6ab02822), the reviewer demo login's server route (6ab02884), Manage billing on the web (6ab189bb), the push credentials (NP-063) and the processor list in the Privacy Policy (6ab028bd) are all To do or in progress outside this epic.
2. **The web ships daily** (about 680 commits since 2026-05-31 against 4 native). Until the contract test (NP-016) lands, any web change can break the native app silently; copied pure logic drifts unless the lockstep tests (NP-017 decides the mechanism) run in the web's CI.
3. **Green tests are not a working app.** 620 passing tests coexist with an app that cannot bundle, 20 test-only modules and inert push, health, biometrics and offline code. Every wave ends in a device demo for this reason.
4. **Beta shares production's database.** A development or preview build pointed at beta writes real member data. Until NP-011, NP-083, NP-074, NP-069 land, builds that reach those paths are used only with named test accounts.
5. **The timezone contract** (NP-009) must land before anyone uses a native build: until then every native write lands on the UTC day and native workout saves record no zone, which also keys reminders and allowance windows.
6. **Sessions are not revoked server-side.** `verifyAuth` (`webapp/lib/auth.ts:132-165`) never reads the user row, so native sign-out and deletion must leave nothing on the device (NP-047), and a token on another device keeps working (open question).
7. **External purchase rules are narrow.** Selling by link is allowed only on the US storefront, availability must be US-only, and the rule has changed twice in two years. A same-domain universal link tapped in Safari stays in Safari, so the return from checkout needs the `become://` scheme or a link on the other domain (NP-051).
8. **Native modules need development builds.** Camera, speech, HealthKit, widgets and Live Activities cannot be tested in Expo Go; the first device build (NP-008) gates most of wave 2.
9. **The allowance-ticket rule is easy to break silently.** A native photo estimate that drops the follow-up ticket makes a free member's first correction cost tomorrow's scan (NP-090).
10. **Moving web targets** (section 9): porting before the trial, the tutorial decline, Manage billing, the cardio layout, the summary metrics or The Becoming settle means porting twice.
11. **Dark-only v1** (NP-013) may not be what Jon expects; light mode is NP-123, 33 files.
12. **The chat API has no role gate**, and every chat message pushes to `/dashboard/chat`. Hiding chat natively (NP-032) does not make the API safe.
13. **The live-workout draft sits in the secure store**, which warns above 2 KB per value; a long workout's draft can fail to save until NP-079 moves it.
14. **The v1 scope is large:** 122 tickets. If the date matters more than parity, the P2 v1 tickets are the first to move after the release (open questions).
15. **SDK cadence.** After the move to SDK 57, plan an SDK upgrade every half year, or EAS build images stop supporting the app.

## 11. Open questions

1. Answered 9/28 (George): the native app ships on both stores, replacing the iOS wrapper and the Android Trusted Web Activity. NP-201 (the Play release), NP-205 (moving web-app members to the app) and the FCM credentials in NP-063 are in v1; the Android widgets, Health Connect and the ongoing workout notification (NP-198 to NP-200) follow in wave 5 with their iOS counterparts.
2. v1 sign-in methods (NP-025): the email link plus the reviewer code, or Google too and therefore Sign in with Apple at launch? (George, Jon)
3. Push transport (NP-030): the Expo push service (fits the existing model and the policy text, adds Expo as a processor) or direct APNs? (George)
4. Crash reporting provider (NP-039): Sentry or an in-house endpoint? Either way it becomes a declared Diagnostics data type and a SECURITY_PROGRAM.md provider. (George)
5. How web and native share pure logic (NP-017): copies inside expo checked by a webapp drift test (recommended), a shared package with a widened build context, or a published package? (George)
6. Unported surfaces in v1 (NP-012): hide them (recommended, with one admin-only web link), or open the web, which makes the signed-in hand-off (NP-121) a v1 ticket? (George, Jon)
7. Answered 10/13 (Jon, NP-028): (a) PR meaning — "the PR is a personal record for all time": the summary's "new personal record" means the server's all-time record (`newPRsAchieved`), not merely beating the previous session; both apps show the same all-time PR. The web's session-only compare at `WorkoutSummary.tsx:106-120` changes first — carded as NP-208 (Wave 0, Store v1: yes) ahead of NP-086. (b) kg labels — "the KG members should be able to use KG throughout the whole app": kg members see kg on every lifting load, not lbs, which needs a server decision on how set weights are stored; the web's lb-only `dumbbellWeight.ts` changes first — carded as NP-209 (Wave 0, Store v1: yes) ahead of NP-080 and NP-084. A member who has not chosen lb or kg is asked which one they want ("yes you should be asked if you dont pick 'lb or Kg'"). (c) the locked-phone rest alert — sound or silent: narrowed 10/13 (Jon, NP-028, second reply): "there should be the locked screen rest timer ... giving both sound and silent vibrations when the timer is done[,] for both live and track" — the alert gives both sound and vibration when the rest ends, on the lock screen, in Live and in Track alike (Track gets a rest timer for the first time). The web's vibrate-only Live alert and Track's missing rest timer change first — carded as NP-210 (Wave 0, Store v1: yes) ahead of NP-082. Whether the first rest may ask for notification permission when the member skipped the ask after onboarding (NP-065) is still NOT answered; NP-082 stays blocked on this one remaining sub-question, carried forward as question 25. (d) Track and Live — "track and live review should be the same in the native app just like the webapp": the first store release ships both Track and Live, reviewed the same way the web does (NP-087).
8. Is a dark-only v1 acceptable (NP-013), or must light mode (NP-123, 33 files) ship at launch? (Jon)
9. Returning from Safari after paying (NP-051): the become:// scheme (Safari asks once) or a return page on becomeurbest.com that links to a claimed become.redbtn.io path? (George)
10. Which domain do native web links and shared links use, becomeurbest.com or become.redbtn.io? The app associates only become.redbtn.io today; NP-044 adds becomeurbest.com if links are built on it. (George)
11. Answered (NP-129): A Stripe trial with a card, following NP-052 rules (Safari, US storefront only). Card is charged after 10 days unless cancelled.
12. Answered 10/8 (Jon, NP-164): choice (b) — a short native coach-mark tour with its own progress key. One simple tour that brings the member through each main hub of our app (just the basics), with an explicit choice to decline so as not to be annoying. Carded in NP-207 (Wave 4, Store v1: no, Size M, P3) with its own progress key (`native-onboarding`), strictly never writing the web tour's progress key (`become-onboarding`). No native screen waits on tour state.
13. Should the reflection after a Mind session run silently, so a member who declined AI is not shown the consent sheet at the end of every session with answers (NP-101)? If yes, both apps change together. (Jon)
14. Should the server stop sending chat pushes to members who cannot open chat (the web shows 'Coming soon', native hides chat)? NP-064 already skips native devices while chat is hidden. (Jon)
15. Is the Mind mirror scene (front camera) wanted in the first store build, adding a camera purpose for Mind to App Review, or should mirror moves use the speak fallback until later (NP-100)? (Jon)
16. The Becoming (NP-203): a native journey stage (Tier 2, weeks of gesture work) or a vertical weekly story that reads the same? Decide after using NP-192 on a phone. (Jon)
17. Should the web also run the Auto meal-plan promotion on the nutrition day view? Today it runs only in the timeline's day view; the native port runs it on its one day view (NP-147). (Jon)
18. Should the streak milestone modal appear after any log that crosses a milestone, on both apps (NP-159)? The web shows it only after changing mood on the mood tile. (Jon)
19. Should the legacy Journal, Meditation and Sleep tabs come back, and should the Arsenal page be linked again? Nothing on the live web shows them, and the native port follows the answer (NP-153). (Jon)
20. Is AI label-photo lookup (`POST /api/ai/nutrition/product`, never surfaced on the web) wanted anywhere? (Jon)
21. Session revocation: the store deletion feature (6aa2e122) is marked done with session revocation ticked, but `verifyAuth` (`webapp/lib/auth.ts:132-165`) never reads the user row, so a token on a second device keeps working through the 7-day grace and a signed-out token for its 30 days. Add a stored token version (NP-180 needs one for the widgets token anyway), or record that clients signing themselves out is enough? (George)
22. NP-029 closes the `POST /api/share` hole going forward. Should George run a read-only query for existing public snapshots of custom programs made by someone other than the owner? (George)
23. Done 9/29: the store epic's route paragraph and order of work now name the native app for both stores, store stories 6ab02897 and 6ab02891 are archived, and the TWA feature and its stories carry a superseded note.
24. A leaner v1 if the date matters more than parity: v1 is 122 tickets and about 192 engineer-days. The first candidates to move after the store release are the P2 v1 tickets (NP-070, NP-111, NP-112, NP-113, NP-114, NP-115). (George, Jon)
25. Narrowed 10/13 (Jon, NP-028, second reply): the locked-phone rest alert gives both sound and vibration when the timer ends, in Live and in Track alike — answered, carded as NP-210. Still open: whether the first rest may ask for notification permission when the member skipped the ask after onboarding (NP-065)? NP-082 cannot be finalized until this one sub-question is answered. (Jon)

## 12. Keeping this document current

- **The matrix is the source for the parity checklist.** NP-116 writes `expo/PARITY.md` from section 5 for Jon's sign-off and retires the early gap catalogue and its format test. After that, `PARITY.md` is the sign-off record and this document the analysis behind it.
- **Update the row with the ticket.** A PR that lands a native parity ticket changes the Status and Tickets cells of the rows it touches, in the same PR.
- **Let the contract test tell you when the web moved.** When NP-016 fails on a web PR, the author updates the shared schema, and the matrix row if a native screen changes behaviour.
- **Re-audit on a clock.** Re-run section 4's checks and re-read the matrix before each store submission and after each Expo SDK upgrade. Change the baseline commit and date at the top when you do, and note what moved.
- **Keep the keys.** Ticket keys (NP-001 to NP-206) are stable once the board is filled; new gaps get the next free key and a row here.
- **A re-audit says what it covered, and what it did not.** The 2026-10-14 pass re-read sections 2 and 3 against `226c0df9`, changed the baseline and the "what moved" note at the top, and corrected the route-group paths in section 5 — and says, in section 5 itself, that it did not touch that section's Status cells. A re-audit that silently covers half the document is how a stale page keeps being believed. The outstanding work that pass left was: section 5's 180 rows, section 4's commands (last run 2026-09-28), section 13's index (it stopped at NP-210 while the tree carries keys to NP-382), and the paths in sections 6-11. **The second pass of 2026-10-14 (card `6acf6999eabd988f96d40e77`) closed the first three** — every section 5 cell re-checked against `b405bc1e` with a machine-checked `path:line`, the tally recomputed from the cells, section 4 re-run with today's numbers beside the 2026-09-28 ones, and section 13's index regenerated from the tree to NP-382 — and **deliberately did not touch sections 1, 6 to 11**: their paths and claims are still the 2026-09-28 audit's, section 6 still measures the shared client against `82f8b4bc`, and section 9's waves still read as if NP-207, NP-203 and NP-204 were unbuilt, which the tree contradicts. That is the next re-audit's job, and this is it being said out loud rather than left to be discovered.

### Keys above NP-210, regenerated from the tree on 2026-10-14

The table above is the board's index for NP-001 to NP-210, written on 2026-09-29, and it is left exactly as it was: its
Wave, Store v1, Size, Priority and Story columns are **board** facts, not tree facts. It stopped at NP-210 while the
repository had moved far past it, which is what headline finding 12 means by "the size of the job cannot be read from
this document any more".

This table is the missing half, and it is generated rather than written: a grep for `NP-[0-9]{3}` over `expo/`
(excluding this document, so the file can never be its own evidence), `shared/`, `webapp/`, `.github/` and `scripts/`,
ignoring `dist/` and `node_modules/`, on `b405bc1e` (2026-10-14). What it found:

- **339 distinct NP keys** appear in the tree. **173** of NP-001 to NP-210 do (the other 37 are board tickets whose work
  left no key in the code), and **166** are above NP-210.
- **NP-382 is the highest key in the tree** (`expo/components/programs/ProgramDetail.tsx:631`), so the index now runs to it.
- Six numbers in that range appear nowhere in the tree — **NP-215, NP-217, NP-223, NP-273, NP-335 and NP-350** — and are
  not invented here.
- **81 of the 166** have a `card-NP-xxx-…` acceptance test beside the code; fourteen (NP-221, NP-225, NP-237, NP-257,
  NP-290, NP-300, NP-329, NP-339, NP-356, NP-359, NP-360, NP-366, NP-376, NP-380) appear only in a test, because the code
  they changed carries no key stamp.
- **Wave, Store v1, Size, Priority and Story are deliberately absent.** They live on the board, and the audit container
  has a depth-1 clone and no board access; guessing them is exactly the failure this re-audit exists to undo. What the
  tree can say is where a key lives, how many files mention it, whether a card test holds it, and the line that names it
  — which is what the columns below are. A supervisor can fill the board columns in from the epic.
